members are killed in a Houthi attack hit a cargo vessel in the Bab el-Mandeb.[^cite_note-223] On 13 August, the Houthis announced that they launched drone strikes on the Aramco refinery in Jazan.[^cite_note-224] A Houthi attack along the al‑Mafalis front, south of [Hayfan district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hayfan_district) was repelled by government soldiers.[^cite_note-225] The Bab al-Mandab was targeted by Houthi missiles.[^cite_note-226] Saudi Arabia announced the beginning of the Red Sea defence coalition led by the country.[^cite_note-227] On 14 August, the Houthis launched drone strikes on an Aramco facility in [Najran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najran).[^cite_note-228] Six people, including two government soldiers were killed in a Houthi attack on Mokha.[^cite_note-229] On 15 August, at least one security guard was killed, eight others were injured and two sailors went missing in a Houthi ballistic missile attack on the Mokha port.[^cite_note-230] On 17 August, the Houthis claimed to have struck a Saudi military ship and four escort vessels near Mokha.[^cite_note-231] On 18 August, the Houthis fired drones towards an Aramco oil refinery in Jazan.[^cite_note-232] On 20 August, the Houthis stated that they targeted Najran Regional Airport and an Aramco facility in Najran,[^cite_note-233] And on 24 August, the Houthis stated they struck a vessel near [Yanbu](629638.md).[^cite_note-234]

#### Renewed fighting

Main articles: [Saudi-led intervention in the Yemeni civil war](46230181.md) and [2026 Yemen offensives](84167596.md)

On 3 September 2026, the Houthis launched a general offensive in coastal regions for control of the [Bab-el-Mandeb](62138.md); 129 deaths were reported.[^cite_note-235] On 4 September, the Houthis attacked various positions near Taiz.[^cite_note-236] On 5 September, fighting broke out near [Hays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hais,_Yemen), and a missile strike on Taiz killed 6 people and wounded 7 others. A Yemeni military officer said the "coming hours will be decisive".[^cite_note-237] On 6 September, government forces stated that they retook [Hays district](29273934.md).[^cite_note-238] On 7 September, the Houthis accused Saudi Arabia of conducting air assaults in [Al-Jawf Governorate](3604364.md), [Al-Bayda Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Bayda_Governorate), [Marib Governorate](3604391.md) and [Taiz Governorate](3604497.md), adding that seven people, including a child were killed and at least seven others, including a woman were injured in one of those attacks that hit a jail in [Al Hazm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Hazm,_Yemen).[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240] On 8 September, the Houthis launched missile and drone [attacks](84173141.md) on civilian, economic and military facilities in [Abha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abha), [Jizan](1907222.md), [Najran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najran) and [Khamis Mushait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khamis_Mushait), wounding 73 people, including women and children, and igniting fires at oil facilities and utilities in southern Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-241] The government forces launched a counteroffensive against the Houthis, with the goal of recapturing [Sanaa](18691665.md) from the group.[^cite_note-242] On 9 September, the Houthis reported more than 30 Saudi attacks on [Marib](3604391.md), [Al-Jawf](3604364.md), [Taiz](3604497.md) and [Al-Hudaydah Governorates](3604362.md).[^cite_note-243] On 10 September, the Houthis reported roughly 40 Saudi attacks on Marib, Al-Jawf, Taiz and Al-Hudaydah Governorates.[^cite_note-244] The Houthis [recaptured Mokha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Mocha_\(2026\)).[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246] On 11 September, the Houthis completed their takeover of the Bab el-Mandeb region by recapturing [Perim](1907645.md) Island, opening a new front in the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md).[^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248] [Al-Masirah](49486469.md) reported two Saudi attacks on Mokha airport.[^cite_note-249] The Houthis completed their seizure of entire [Red Sea](26191.md) coast by recapturing the [Hanish Islands](3542308.md).[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251] On 13 September, two people are injured in a Houthi attack in [Jazan Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazan_Province).[^cite_note-252] The Houthis declared that they launched a missile and drone attack on [Sharurah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharurah) military base.[^cite_note-253] Al-Masirah reported two Saudi attacks on [Saada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saada).[^cite_note-254] On 14 September, the Houthis announced that they struck [King Khalid Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Khalid_Air_Base).[^cite_note-255] On 15 September, the Saudi-led coalition in Yemen announced that Houthi attacks wounded 13 civilians in Khamis Mushait, Abha and [Taif](201969.md) the day before.[^cite_note-256] The Saudi-led coalition stated that they downed a Houthi drone south of [Mecca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mecca). The Houthis denied the allegation.[^cite_note-257][^cite_note-258] On 16 September, the Houthis stated that they downed a Saudi [F15](11715.md) over [Marib Governorate](3604391.md).[^cite_note-259] The Houthis announced that they targeted King Khalid Air Base and [Aramco](290527.md) utilities in [Yanbu](629638.md).[^cite_note-260] On 17 September, Al-Masirah reported that Saudi attacks targeted three telecommunications towers in [Taiz Governorate](3604497.md), while one civilian was killed and another wounded in attacks on [Abs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abs,_Yemen).[^cite_note-261] [Saudi state TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Broadcasting_Authority) reported that one person was killed and two others were wounded amidst a drone downing in [Taif](201969.md).[^cite_note-262] Al-Masirah reported that government attacks in Taiz Governorate killed three children and wounded several others.[^cite_note-263] On 18 September, an Italian [Eurofighter Typhoon](167667.md) jet was damaged by the Houthis in Saudi Arabia after Italy announced that it will deploy warships to protect shipping through Bab al-Mandeb.[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] On 19 September, the Houthis stated that they targeted "sensitive sites" in Riyadh and Aramco utilities in Yanbu.[^cite_note-266] The Saudi-led coalition stated it downed a Houthi ballistic missile launched at Riyadh.[^cite_note-267] On 22 September, in order to combat Houthi attacks, British Prime Minister [Andy Burnham](414818.md) announced that the [Royal Air Force](25679.md) would provide air-to-air refueling for Saudi aircraft.[^cite_note-268] [Saba News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saba_News_Agency) announced that six civilians, including two children and a woman, were killed and eight others, including a child and a woman were wounded in Saudi attacks in Mokha.[^cite_note-269] On 23 September, government soldiers claimed that they captured Mount Qarfan in Taiz Governorate.[^cite_note-270] On 24 September, the government claimed to have thwarted a Houthi attack on a vital supply route to [Aden](38050.md).[^cite_note-271] The Saudi-led coalition announced that it downed six ballistic missiles launched by the Houthis towards Taif and Yanbu.[^cite_note-272] The Houthis stated that they struck "a sensitive target" in Riyadh and Aramco infrastructure in Yanbu.[^cite_note-273] French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) announced that the country will send soldiers to [protect](52395034.md) Saudi Arabia on a Red Sea oil route, as part of an accord with Riyadh.[^cite_note-274] On 25 September, President [Rashad al-Alimi](70502008.md) called on Yemenis to join the armed forces against the Houthis and promised a general amnesty for Houthi fighters and affiliates who leave the group beginning the next day.[^cite_note-275] On 26 September, the Saudi-led coalition stated that it had downed two missiles and two drones ‌fired by the Houthis at Khamis Mushait and Riyadh.[^cite_note-276] On 27 September, the Houthi-run health ministry stated that an attack on a market in the Mawiya junction of [Taiz Governorate](3604497.md) killed seven people ​and wounded ​40 others, including children. The government stated that its targets were a Houthi military camp and vehicles at the same area.[^cite_note-277] On 1 October, the Saudi-led coalition stated that the Houthis struck a power station providing electricity to the [Prophet's Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prophet's_Mosque) in [Medina](36636.md), one of [Muslim's holiest sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holiest_sites_in_Islam), causing one transformer to went out of service, ‌a claim denied by the group.[^cite_note-278] The Saudi-led coalition reported that they downed four Houthi drones and two ballistic missiles fired at Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-279] Houthi media declared that the [Saudi Arabian Armed Forces](27276.md) struck [Sanaa](18691665.md) for the first time in years.[^cite_note-280] On 2 October, the [Saba News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saba_News_Agency) reported Saudi attacks in [Sanaa](18691665.md).[^cite_note-281] The [Yemeni Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemeni_Air_Force) announced that it conducted 20 strikes on Houthi targets in [Taiz Governorate](3604497.md).[^cite_note-282] The Houthis stated that Saudi Arabia conducted 94 attacks in parts of Yemen under their governance in the last day.[^cite_note-283] The Saudi coalition announced that they downed three ballistic missiles fired by the Houthis at [Khamis Mushait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khamis_Mushait).[^cite_note-284] On 3 October, Saudi authorities announced that [shrapnel](37345.md) from a downed Houthi ballistic ballistic missile wounded one individual in [Ahad Rafidah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahad_Rafidah).[^cite_note-285] [Al-Masirah](49486469.md) announced that Saudi Arabia conducted 24 attacks on Sanaa.[^cite_note-286] Government forces stated that they struck "legitimate vital military targets in Sanaa”.[^cite_note-287]

## Foreign involvement

Main article: [Foreign involvement in the Yemeni civil war (2014–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_involvement_in_the_Yemeni_civil_war_\(2014%E2%80%93present\))

### Houthi allies

In April 2015, [United States National Security Council](44026.md) spokeswoman [Bernadette Meehan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernadette_Meehan) stated that: "It remains our assessment that Iran does not exert command and control over the Houthis in Yemen".[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289]

The United States has regularly accused the Iranian government of arming and funding the Houthis. In an April 2015 interview with the [PBS Newshour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PBS_NewsHour), then-[U.S. secretary of state](32293.md) [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) accused Iran of attempting to destabilize Yemen.[^cite_note-warns-290] While the Houthis and the Iranian government have denied any military affiliation,[^cite_note-crucial-291] Iranian supreme leader [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) openly announced his "spiritual" support of the movement in a personal meeting with the Houthi spokesperson Mohammed Abdul Salam in [Tehran](57654.md), in the midst of ongoing conflicts in Aden in 2019.[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293]

Although there has been no direct intervention by the Iranian government, the Yemeni civil war is widely regarded as part of the [Iran-Saudi proxy conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_proxy_conflict).[^cite_note-294] The United States, Saudi Arabia, the UAE and various Western commentators have accused various [IRGC](1285472.md) networks of assisting Houthis through arms supplies, military training, logistics, strategic co-ordination and media support.[^cite_note-csis.org-295][^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297] Saudi Arabia views activities by the [Quds Forces](4028355.md) and [Hezbollah](13919.md) in neighboring Yemen as part of Iranian attempts to establish a [satellite state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satellite_state) in the country and trap them into a stalemate.[^cite_note-BBC-20222-115][^cite_note-298] Western commentators have argued that the Iranian policy in Yemen has been hinged on developing bases for ballistic missiles targeting [GCC](220950.md) countries, establishing naval dominance in the strategic [Bab al-Mandeb](62138.md) Strait by advancing [area denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-access/area_denial) capabilities and [weapons trafficking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weapons_trafficking), in addition to the intensification of [Iranian cyberwarfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_cyberwarfare).[^cite_note-csis.org-295][^cite_note-299] However, this characterization has been disputed by various analysts and academics, who assert that Houthis are independent of Iran. According to professor [Stephen Zunes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Zunes), the majority of Houthi arms supplies primarily originate from the [black market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_market), and Houthis obtain most of their weaponry from non-Iranian sources.[^cite_note-300]

On 7 August 2018, [IRGC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRGC) commander [Nasser Shabani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Shabani) was quoted by the [Fars News Agency](5000099.md), the semi-official news agency of the Iranian government, as saying, "We (IRGC) told Yemenis \[Houthi rebels] to strike two Saudi oil tankers, and they did it".[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302] *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* reported in March 2023 that the Iranian government agreed to halt all military support to Houthis and abide by the [UN arms embargo](5974443.md), as part of a [Chinese](5405.md)-brokered [Iran-Saudi rapprochement deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_proxy_conflict#Iran-Saudi_Arabia_Rapprochement).[^cite_note-303]

The [Eritrean](17238590.md) government has also been accused of funneling Iranian material to the Houthis,[^cite_note-tjotw-304] as well as offering medical care for injured Houthi fighters.[^cite_note-alarm-305] However, it has called the allegations "groundless" and said after the outbreak of open hostilities that it views the Yemeni crisis "as an internal matter".[^cite_note-tjotw-304] According to a secret [United Nations](31769.md) document, [North Korea](21255.md) had aided the Houthis by selling weapons.[^cite_note-North_Korean_weapons_aid-306][^cite_note-North_Korean_aid-307]

### Yemeni government supporters

The Yemeni government meanwhile has enjoyed significant international backing from the United States and Persian Gulf monarchies after requesting their support.[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310] [U.S. drone strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_drone_strikes_in_Yemen) were conducted regularly in Yemen during Hadi's presidency in [Sanaa](18691665.md), usually targeting [Al Qaeda in the Arabian Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Qaeda_in_the_Arabian_Peninsula).[^cite_note-311] The United States was also a major supplier of weapons to the Yemeni government, although according to the [Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon), hundreds of millions of [dollars](18717338.md)' worth of that material has gone missing since it was delivered.[^cite_note-loses-312] Saudi Arabia provided financial aid to Yemen until late 2014, when it suspended it amid the Houthis' takeover of Sanaa and increasing influence over the Yemeni government.[^cite_note-313] According to [Amnesty International](18947898.md), the [United Kingdom](31717.md) also supplied weaponry used by Saudi-led coalition to strike targets in Yemen.[^cite_note-314] Amnesty International also says that U.S.-based [Raytheon Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raytheon) supplied a laser-guided bomb that killed six civilians on 28 June 2019.[^cite_note-315]

In May 2019, State Secretary [Mike Pompeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Pompeo) announced an "emergency" to push through \$8.1 billion of arms sales to Gulf allies like Saudi Arabia, the United Arab Emirates, and Jordan, citing Iranian activity in the Middle East. Despite widespread congressional opposition, an August 2020 report by the Office of Inspector General detailed that Pompeo complied with the legal prerequisites to do so. The report, however, also noted that the possible threat to the lives of the civilians caught in the crossfire was not assessed properly at the time of the emergency. The State Department was also accused in the report of violating the threshold of the [Arms Export Control Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_Export_Control_Act) while approving arms sales to Gulf states.[^cite_note-316]

### Saudi-led intervention in Yemen



A pro-Saleh protest in [Sanaa](18691665.md) against the Saudi-led intervention, March 2016

Main article: [Saudi-led intervention in the Yemeni civil war](46230181.md)

In response to rumors that [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) could intervene in Yemen, Houthi commander Ali al-Shami boasted on 24 March 2015 that his forces would invade the larger kingdom and not stop at [Mecca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mecca), but rather [Riyadh](51554.md).[^cite_note-317]

The following evening, answering a request by Yemen's internationally recognized government, Saudi Arabia began a military intervention alongside eight other Arab States (United Arab Emirates, Egypt, Jordan, Bahrain, Kuwait, Qatar, Morocco, and Sudan)[^cite_note-318] and Pakistan. The [United States](3434750.md) also helped attack the Houthis – bombing positions throughout Sanaa. In a joint statement, the nations of the [Gulf Cooperation Council](220950.md) (with the exception of [Oman](22316.md)) said they decided to intervene against the Houthis in Yemen at the request of Hadi's government.[^cite_note-reuters.com-319][^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321] [King Salman of Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salman_of_Saudi_Arabia) declared the [Royal Saudi Air Force](570367.md) to be in full control of Yemeni airspace within hours of the operation beginning.[^cite_note-repel-48] The airstrikes were aimed at hindering the Houthis' advance toward Hadi's stronghold in southern Yemen.[^cite_note-322]

[Al Jazeera](30204052.md) reported that [Mohammed Ali al-Houthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Ali_al-Houthi), a Houthi commander appointed in February as President of the [Revolutionary Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary_Committee_\(Yemen\)), was injured by an airstrike in Sanaa on the first night of the campaign.[^cite_note-323]

After the launch of Operation [Decisive Storm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decisive_Storm), Saudi Arabia along with the UAE used lobbying in an attempt to rally the international community to their cause.[^cite_note-\:5-132]

[Reuters](18998750.md) reported that planes from [Egypt](8087628.md), [Morocco](19291.md), [Jordan](7515964.md), [Sudan](27421.md), [Kuwait](7515890.md), [United Arab Emirates](69328.md), [Qatar](8238258.md), and [Bahrain](18933277.md) had also taken part in the operation.[^cite_note-offensive-107] Saudi Arabia requested that [Pakistan](23235.md) commit forces as well, but [Pakistan's parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Pakistan) officially voted to remain neutral.[^cite_note-324] However, Pakistan agreed to provide support in line with a [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) resolution, dispatching warships to enforce an [arms embargo](5974443.md) against the Houthis.[^cite_note-repel-48][^cite_note-325]

On 21 April 2015, the bombing campaign was officially declared over, with Saudi officials saying they would begin *Operation Restoring Hope* as a combination of political, diplomatic, and military efforts to end the war.[^cite_note-326] Even still, airstrikes continued against Houthi targets, and fighting in Aden and [Ad Dali'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ad_Dali') went on.[^cite_note-327]

The United Arab Emirates claimed to take an active role against fighting [AQAP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda_in_the_Arabian_Peninsula) and [IS-YP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State_%E2%80%93_Yemen_Province) presence in Yemen through a partnership with the United States. In an Op-Ed in *[The Washington Post](102226.md),* [Yousef Al Otaiba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yousef_Al_Otaiba), the UAE ambassador to the United States, described that the intervention has reduced [AQAP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda_in_the_Arabian_Peninsula) presence in Yemen to its weakest point since 2012 with many areas previously under their control liberated.[^cite_note-WP-328] The ambassador claimed that more than 2,000 militants have been removed from the battlefield, with their controlled areas now having improved security and a better delivered humanitarian and development assistance such as to the port city of [Mukalla](2414800.md) and other liberated areas.[^cite_note-WP-328]

An [Associated Press](18935732.md) investigation outlined that the military coalition in Yemen actively reduced AQAP in Yemen without military intervention, instead of by offering them deals and even actively recruiting them in the coalition because "they're considered exceptional fighters".[^cite_note-329] UAE Brigadier General Musallam Al Rashidi responded to the accusations by stating that Al Qaeda cannot be reasoned with and citing that they killed many of his soldiers.[^cite_note-330] The UAE military stated that accusations of allowing AQAP to leave with cash contradict their primary objective of depriving AQAP of its financial strength.[^cite_note-TIDx-331] The notion of the coalition recruiting or paying AQAP has been thoroughly denied by the United States [Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon), with spokesperson Robert Manning calling the news source "patently false".[^cite_note-332] The governor of Hadramut [Faraj al-Bahsani](71436343.md), dismissed the accusations that Al Qaeda has joined with the coalition ranks, explaining that if they did there would be sleeper cells and that he would be "the first one to be killed". According to *The Independent*, AQAP activity on social media as well as the number of terror attacks conducted by them has decreased since the Emirati intervention.[^cite_note-TIDx-331]

On 13 July 2020, the UK [Ministry of Defence](203793.md) logged more than 500 Saudi air raids in possible breach of international law in Yemen. These figures were revealed a few days after the [UK government](25318118.md) decided to resume arms sales to Saudi Arabia, which could be used in the Yemen war, just over a year after the court of appeal ruled them unlawful. It justified resuming arms sales stating that only isolated incidents without any pattern have occurred.[^cite_note-333]

In March 2019, the [United States Congress](31756.md) voted to end US support to the Saudi war effort;[^cite_note-334] however, [US President](24113.md) [Donald Trump](4848272.md) [vetoed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veto_power_in_the_United_States) it.[^cite_note-335] His successor, President [Joe Biden](145422.md), announced a freeze on arms sales to Saudi Arabia and the UAE in January 2021.[^cite_note-336] He also declared that he would end American support for the Saudi coalition.[^cite_note-337] On 4 February 2021, Biden announced an end to the [US support for Saudi-led operations in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._support_for_Saudi-led_operations_in_Yemen).[^cite_note-338] As of 13 April 2021, arms sales to the UAE had not been stopped, while sales to Saudi Arabia were still paused.[^cite_note-339] However, details of the end of American involvement in the war were not released as of 2021.[^cite_note-340]

In August 2020, confidential Saudi documents revealed the kingdom's strategy in Yemen. The 162 pages of classified Saudi documents dated back prior to 2015. The pages showed the kingdom's inaction in stopping the Houthis from capturing Sanaa, despite Saudi intelligence reports. The records also exposed that Saudi Arabia had worked to hamper the rebuilding projects commissioned by [Germany](11867.md) and Qatar in Saada. This happened after the fighting between the two warring camps came to a halt due to a ceasefire.[^cite_note-341]

### Arab League

In [Egypt](8087628.md), the Yemeni foreign minister called in 2015 for an [Arab League](52625.md) military intervention against the Houthis.[^cite_note-reuters.com-319] Egyptian President [Abdel Fattah al-Sisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdel_Fattah_al-Sisi) floated the idea of a unified military force.[^cite_note-342]

The Arab League announced the formation of a unified military force to respond to conflict in Yemen and Libya in March 2015.[^cite_note-343]

### US role

See also: [Terrorism in Yemen § US air attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_Yemen#US_air_attacks), and [Drone strikes in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drone_strikes_in_Yemen)

Since the mid-2000s, the [United States](3434750.md) has been carrying out targeted killings of [Al-Qaeda](1921.md) militants in [Yemen](350939.md), although the U.S. government generally does not confirm involvement in specific attacks conducted by [unmanned aerial vehicles](58900.md) as a matter of policy. The [Bureau of Investigative Journalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Investigative_Journalism) documented 415 strikes in Pakistan and Yemen by 2015 since the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md), and according to the organization's estimates, between 423 and 962 deaths are believed to have been civilians. [Michael Morell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Morell), former deputy director of the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA), claims the numbers were significantly lower.[^cite_note-344]

During the civil war in Yemen, drone strikes have continued, targeting suspected leaders of [Al Qaeda in the Arabian Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Qaeda_in_the_Arabian_Peninsula). [Ibrahim al-Rubeish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibrahim_al-Rubeish) and [Nasser bin Ali al-Ansi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_bin_Ali_al-Ansi), two leading AQAP figures, were killed by U.S. drone strikes in the vicinity of [Mukalla](2414800.md) in 2015.[^cite_note-ansi-345][^cite_note-346] Approximately 240 suspected AQAP militants have been killed by American drone strikes since the civil war began.[^cite_note-347]

In 2013, [Radhya Al-Mutawakel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radhya_Al-Mutawakel) and Abdelrasheed Al-Faqih, Directors of Mwatana, published a joint report with [Open Society Foundations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Society_Foundations) titled 'Death by Drone', detailing evidence of civilian casualties and damage to civilian objects in nine US drone strikes.[^cite_note-348]

The United States supplied intelligence and logistical support, including aerial refueling and search and rescue for downed coalition pilots.[^cite_note-hp-20160810-349] The US also revived arms sales to coalition countries.[^cite_note-350] In January 2016, [Saudi Arabia's foreign minister](31639195.md) stated: US and UK military officials were in the command and control center responsible for the Saudi-led airstrikes in Yemen and had access to the list of targets but were not involved in the selection of targets.[^cite_note-theguardian-com_2016-01-15_BZM-351][^cite_note-theguardian-com_2016-01-19_BHS-352][^cite_note-publications-parliament-uk_2016-01-12_HoC-353]

In March 2025, the United States launched [air and naval attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2025_United_States_attacks_in_Yemen) against dozens of Houthi targets in Yemen, the largest military operation in the Middle East of United States President [Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s [second term](78298108.md).[^cite_note-Reuters20250315-354] US officials said that the first wave of attacks, launched on 15 March against radars, air defenses, and missile and drone systems, were the first in a wider effort to end [Houthi attacks](75207766.md) on commercial vessels and warships in the [Red Sea](26191.md) and [Gulf of Aden](58716.md).[^cite_note-NYTimes20250315-355]

### Islamic State presence and operations

The Islamic State (IS) has proclaimed several provinces in Yemen and has urged its adherents to wage war against the Houthi movement, as well as against Zaydis in general.[^cite_note-356] ISIS militants have conducted bombing attacks in various parts of the country, particularly against mosques in Sanaa.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358]

On 6 October 2015, IS militants conducted a [series of suicide bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2015_Aden_missile_attack) in Aden that killed 15 soldiers affiliated with the Hadi-led government and the Saudi-led coalition.[^cite_note-reutersoct6-359] The attacks were directed against the al-Qasr hotel, which had been a headquarters for pro-Hadi officials, and also military facilities.[^cite_note-reutersoct6-359] Yemeni officials and the UAE state news agency declared that 11 Yemeni and 4 United Arab Emirates soldiers were killed in Aden due to 4 coordinated Islamic State suicide bombings. Prior to the claim of responsibility by the Islamic State, UAE officials blamed the Houthis and former president Ali Abdullah Saleh, for the attacks.[^cite_note-reutersoct6-359]

## Humanitarian aspects of the war

Further information: [Blockade of Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blockade_of_Yemen)

See also: [COVID-19 pandemic in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Yemen), [2019–2022 locust infestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932022_locust_infestation), and [Refugees on Jeju Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_on_Jeju_Island)

[CNN](62028.md) reported on 8 April 2015 that almost 10,160,000 Yemenis were deprived of water, food, and electricity as a result of the initial year of the conflict. The report also added per source from [UNICEF](19698110.md) officials in Yemen that within 15 days, some 100,000 people across the country were dislocated, while Oxfam said that more than 10 million Yemenis did not have enough food to eat, in addition to 850,000 half-starved children. Over 13 million civilians were without access to clean water.[^cite_note-360]

A medical aid boat brought 2.5 tonnes of medicine to [Aden](38050.md) on 8 April 2015.[^cite_note-361] A UNICEF plane loaded with 16 tonnes of supplies landed in [Sanaa](18691665.md) on 10 April.[^cite_note-362] The [United Nations](31769.md) announced on 19 April 2015 that [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) promised to provide \$273.7 million in emergency humanitarian aid to Yemen. The UN appealed for aid, saying 7.5 million people had been affected by the conflict and many were in need of medical supplies, potable water, food, shelter, and other forms of support.[^cite_note-363]

On 12 May 2015, [Oxfam](19698439.md) warned that the five days a humanitarian ceasefire was scheduled to last would not be sufficient to fully address Yemen's humanitarian crisis.[^cite_note-trucetakes-364] It was also said that the Houthis were collecting a war tax on goods. The political analyst Abdulghani al-Iryani affirmed that this tax is: "an illegal levy, mostly extortion that is not determined by the law and the amount is at the discretion of the field commanders".[^cite_note-365]

As the war dragged on through the summer and into the fall of 2015, [Cyclone Chapala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyclone_Chapala), the equivalent of a category 2 Hurricane,[^cite_note-366] made landfall on 3 November 2015. According to the [NGO](46539.md) [Save the Children](436885.md), the destruction of healthcare facilities and a healthcare system on the brink of collapse as a result of the war could cause an estimated 10,000 preventable child deaths annually. Some 1,219 children died as a direct result of the conflict by December 2016 according to Edward Santiago, the NGO's Yemen director.[^cite_note-367]

> Even before the war tens of thousands of Yemeni children were dying of preventable causes. But now, the situation is much worse and an estimated 1,000 children are dying every week from preventable killers like diarrhea, malnutrition, and respiratory tract infections.

In March 2017, the World Food Program reported that while Yemen was not yet in a full-blown famine, 60% of Yemenis, or 17 million people, were in "crisis" or "emergency" food situations.[^cite_note-368]

In June 2017, a [cholera epidemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%932021_Yemen_cholera_outbreak) resurfaced which was reported to be killing a person an hour in Yemen by mid June.[^cite_note-369] News reports in mid June stated that there had been 124,000 cases and 900 deaths and that 20 of the 22 provinces in Yemen were affected at that time.[^cite_note-370] [UNICEF](19698110.md) and [WHO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WHO) estimated that, by 24 June 2017, the total cases in the country exceeded 200,000, with 1,300 deaths.[^cite_note-371] 77.7% of cholera cases (339,061 of 436,625) and 80.7% of deaths from cholera (1,545 of 1,915) occurred in [Houthi](17240332.md)-controlled governorates, compared to 15.4% of cases and 10.4% of deaths in government-controlled governorates, since Houthi-controlled areas have been disproportionately affected by the conflict, which has created conditions conducive to the spread of cholera.[^cite_note-372]

On 7 June 2018, the [International Committee of the Red Cross](5023832.md) (ICRC) pulled 71 of its international staff out of Yemen, and moved the rest of them to [Djibouti](17207794.md), with some 450 ICRC employees remaining in the country. The partial evacuation measure came on the eve of an ICRC worker, a Lebanese national, being killed on 21 April by unknown gunmen in the southwestern city of [Taiz](545270.md). The ICRC stated, "our current activities have been blocked, threatened and directly targeted in recent weeks, and we see a vigorous attempt to instrumentalize our organization as a pawn in the conflict." In light of the serious security deterioration for ICRC personnel, the international organization has called for all parties of the conflict "to provide it with concrete, solid and actionable guarantees so that it can continue working in Yemen." *Al Jazeera* reported that more than 10,000 people had been killed and at least 40,000 wounded since the beginning of the conflict, mostly from air raids.[^cite_note-373]



Protest against the war in Yemen on 14 August 2020

The [International Rescue Committee](196452.md) stated in March 2019 that at least 9.8 million people in Yemen were acutely in need of health services. The closure of [Sanaa](4202141.md) and [Riyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riyan_International_Airport) airports for civilian flights and the limited operation of civilian airplanes in government-held areas, made it impossible for most to seek medical treatment abroad. The cost of tickets provided by [Yemenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemenia), [Air Djibouti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Djibouti) and [Queen Bilqis Airways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Bilqis_Airways), also put traveling outside Yemen out of reach for many.[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375]

The [United Nations Development Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme) published a report in September 2019 that said if the war continued, Yemen would become the poorest country in the world, with 79% of the population living below the poverty line and 65% in extreme poverty by 2022.[^cite_note-376]

On 3 December 2019, the International Day of Persons with Disabilities, [Amnesty International](18947898.md) released a report highlighting how the almost 5-year old Yemeni civil war had left millions of people living with disabilities and excluded from medical attention. Amnesty claimed that the armed conflict was led by [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) and [UAE](69328.md) as part of the former's coalition in the Arab nation against [Houthis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_movement) and terror groups, had given birth to the worst humanitarian crisis, as stated by the [United Nations](31769.md).[^cite_note-377]

Humanitarian aid provided to Houthi-controlled Yemen was scaled-down in March 2020 because donors doubt if it's actually reaching the people in need, a UN official said.[^cite_note-378]

In June 2020, the [UNHCR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNHCR) said that following more than five years of war in [Yemen](350939.md), more than 3.6 million people had been forced to flee their homes, while 24 million were in dire need of aid. The group also informed that a significant gap in funding has been recorded with only US\$63 million received thus far, while at least US\$211.9 million is needed to run operations in 2020.[^cite_note-379]

On 2 July 2020, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) reported that detainees at Aden's [Bir Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beir_Ahmed) facility were facing serious health risks from the rapidly spreading [coronavirus pandemic](62750956.md). The informal detention facility, controlled by Yemeni authorities affiliated with the UAE-backed [Southern Transitional Council](54251566.md), is grossly overcrowded and was deprived of health care facilities.[^cite_note-380]

The [World Food Programme](33582.md) (WFP) projected in March 2021 that if the Saudi-led blockade and war continues, more than 400,000 Yemeni children under 5 years old could die from acute malnutrition before the end of the year as the blockade devastates the nation.[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-urlAs_Conflict,_Humanitarian_Crisis_Grows,_Yemen_'Speeding_towards_Massive_Famine',_Under-Secretary-General_Warns,_in_Briefing_to_Security_Council_-_Yemen_|_ReliefWeb-382][^cite_note-urlAt_least_400,000_Yemeni_children_under_5_could_die_of_starvation_this_year_-_UN_agencies_|_Reuters-383]

### War crime accusations

Main article: [War crimes in the Yemeni civil war (2014–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_Yemeni_civil_war_\(2014%E2%80%93present\))



Destroyed house in the south of Sanaa, 13 June 2015

&#x20;According to [Farea Al-Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farea_Al-Muslim), direct war crimes have been committed during the conflict; for example, an [IDP](427408.md) camp was hit by a Saudi airstrike, while Houthis have sometimes prevented aid workers from giving aid.[^cite_note-384] The [UN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN) and several major human rights groups discussed the possibility that war crimes may have been committed by Saudi Arabia during the air campaign.[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386]



Yemeni capital Sanaa after airstrikes, 9 October 2015

British researcher [Alex de Waal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_de_Waal) wrote that the "responsibility for Yemen goes beyond Riyadh and Abu Dhabi to London and Washington. Britain has sold at least £4.5 billion in arms to Saudi Arabia and £500 million to the UAE since the war began. The US role is even bigger: Trump authorized arms sales to the Saudis worth \$110 billion last May. Yemen will be the defining famine crime of this generation, perhaps this century."[^cite_note-387]

In August 2018, a report by UN experts said all parties to the conflict may have committed war crimes, including the governments of Yemen, the United Arab Emirates and Saudi Arabia, and the Houthi movement. The UN described the conflict as the world's worst humanitarian crisis. The report documented 6,475 deaths in the conflict, but estimated the true number was significantly higher. The report criticized Saudi-led airstrikes and accused parties of unlawful violations such as "deprivation of the right to life, arbitrary detention, rape, torture, enforced disappearances and child recruitment".[^cite_note-388]

In February 2020, British law firm Stoke White appealed to the authorities in Britain, the U.S., and Turkey to arrest senior officials from the United Arab Emirates (UAE) for allegedly carrying out [war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UAE_War_Crimes) and torture in Yemen. The complaints were lodged under the provision of 'universal jurisdiction', wherein nations must probe breaches of the Geneva Convention for possible war crimes.[^cite_note-389]

In March 2020, Human Rights Watch accused [Saudi military forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_Saudi_Arabia) and Saudi-backed Yemeni forces of carrying out abuse of Yemeni civilians in the country's eastern province of [al-Mahrah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Mahrah_Governorate). They accused them [arbitrarily arresting](13435705.md), [torturing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torturing), and illegally transferring detainees to the kingdom of Saudi Arabia. The report documented 16 cases of arbitrary detention and five cases of detainees who had been transferred illegally to Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391] According to a report from May 2020 by Yemeni human rights group Mwatana, since May 2016, more than 1,600 cases of arbitrary detentions, 770 forced disappearances, 344 cases of torture and at least 66 deaths in unofficial prisons have been documented. The report stated that Houthis were responsible for most abuses. It blamed them for 350 forced disappearances, 138 incidents of torture, and 27 deaths in detention, while UAE-backed forces, including the Southern Transitional Council, were responsible for 327 disappearances, 141 cases of torture, and 25 deaths in detention. The report blamed forces loyal to the Saudi-backed Yemeni government for 65 cases of torture and over 24 deaths.[^cite_note-392]

An investigation led by [Sky News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_News) reported that on 12 July 2020, an air-strike in Northern Yemen, reportedly carried out by the Saudi-led coalition, hit a family home, killing six children and three women. The investigators found fragments of the bomb and some of the shrapnel which seemed to be the part of a [GBU](3587974.md)-12, 500 lb fin-guided bomb, manufactured in the US. The Joint Incidents Assessment Team is still investigating the attack.[^cite_note-393]

The United Nations has accused both sides of the conflict of using child soldiers. In August 2018, the UN reported that two-thirds of child soldiers in Yemen fought for the Houthis. The report documented 800 cases of child soldiers in 2017.[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-urlExclusive:_Yemeni_child_soldiers_recruited_by_Saudi-UAE_coalition_|_Saudi_Arabia_News_|_Al_Jazeera-395] In December 2018, *The New York Times* reported that Saudi Arabia had hired child soldiers from [Sudan](27421.md) (especially from [Darfur](605837.md)), and Yemen to fight against Houthis. They reported that as many as 14,000 Sudanese militiamen were fighting in the war, many of them children.[^cite_note-urlChild_soldiers_from_Darfur_fighting_at_front_line_of_war_in_Yemen,_returned_soldiers_say_|_The_Independent-396][^cite_note-urlSaudi_Arabia_recruited_Darfur_children_to_fight_in_Yemen:_NYT_|_Saudi_Arabia_News_|_Al_Jazeera-397][^cite_note-urlOn_the_Front_Line_of_the_Saudi_War_in_Yemen:_Child_Soldiers_From_Darfur-398] In March 2019, it was reported that [British SAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Special_Air_Service) special forces had allegedly been involved in training child soldiers in Yemen.[^cite_note-urlSerious_questions_over_SAS_involvement_in_Yemen_war_|_UK_news_|_The_Guardian-399] In March 2019, [Al Jazeera](30204052.md) reported that Saudi Arabia recruited Yemeni children to guard the [Saudi Arabia-Yemen border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia-Yemen_border) against Houthis.[^cite_note-urlExclusive:_Yemeni_child_soldiers_recruited_by_Saudi-UAE_coalition_|_Saudi_Arabia_News_|_Al_Jazeera-395] In January 2022, the UN reported that 1,406 child soldiers recruited by the Houthis had died on the battlefield in 2020 and 562 had died between January and May 2021.[^cite_note-BBC_January_2022-400]

In February 2022, unknown armed men kidnapped five United Nations personnel in southern Yemen as they were returning to Aden after a field tour. According to Russell Geekie, a spokesman for the top UN official in Yemen, the crew was kidnapped on Friday, 11 February 2022, in the Abyan governorate.[^cite_note-401] In August 2023, the kidnapped United Nations personnel were released.[^cite_note-402]

## Impact on citizens

A 2023 Gallup opinion poll found around 71% of Yemenis struggled to afford food and 28% had health problems.[^cite_note-n717-403]

### Children and women



[Code Pink](2440600.md) anti-war activists protest U.S. Senators supporting [Saudi-U.S. arms deals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_United_States%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_arms_deal), December 2017

According to UN estimates, the war has directly caused the deaths of over 3,000 children as of December 2020; while indirect causes of the war (lack of food, health and infrastructure) have led to additional deaths.[^cite_note-2020_UN2-404] The UN estimates that at the end of 2021, 70% of all the casualties of the war (around 259,000) are children under five.[^cite_note-405]

Yemeni refugee women and children are extremely susceptible to smuggling and human trafficking.[^cite_note-406] NGOs report that vulnerable populations in Yemen were at increased risk for human trafficking in 2015 because of ongoing armed conflict, civil unrest, and lawlessness. Migrant workers from Somalia who remained in Yemen during this period suffered from increased violence, and women and children became most vulnerable to human trafficking. Prostitution of women and child sex slaves is a social issue in Yemen. Citizens of other gulf states are beginning to be drawn into the sex tourism industry. The poorest people in Yemen work locally and children are commonly sold as sex slaves abroad. While this issue is worsening, the plight of Somalis in Yemen has been ignored by the government.[^cite_note-407] Emphasizing the dire humanitarian needs of about 23.4 million people, UNFPA reported the lack of access to sexual and reproductive health and protection services, and exposure to death in pregnancy and childbirth and life-threatening violence. An increase in child marriage as a coping mechanism by impoverished families as the conflict continues was also reported.[^cite_note-408]

Children are recruited between the ages of 13 and 17, and as young as 10 years old into the armed forces despite a law against it in 1991. The rate of militant recruitment in Yemen has increased exponentially. According to an international organization, between 26 March and 24 April 2015, armed groups recruited at least 140 children.[^cite_note-409] According to the New York Times report, 1.8 million children in Yemen were extremely subject to malnutrition in 2018.[^cite_note-410]

Both the Saudi-led coalition and the Houthis were blacklisted by the UN over the deaths of children during the war. In 2016 Saudi Arabia was removed from the list after alleged pressure from Gulf countries who threatened to withdraw hundreds of millions of dollars in assistance to the UN, the decision was criticized by human rights groups and the coalition added again in 2017 and was accused of killing or injuring 683 children, and attacking many of schools and hospitals in 38 confirmed attacks, while the Houthis were accused of being responsible for 414 child casualties in 2016.[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412][^cite_note-413] On 16 June 2020, the [United Nations](31769.md) removed the Saudi-led coalition fighting in Yemen war from an annual blacklist of parties violating [children's rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children's_rights). The decision was taken despite the UN finding that the coalition operations killed or injured nearly 222 children in Yemen, in 2019. The Saudi-led coalition's removal from the blacklist leaves Yemeni children vulnerable to future attacks.[^cite_note-414]

In mid-May 2019, a series of Saudi/Emirati-led airstrikes hit Houthi targets on the outskirts of Sanaa. One of the airstrikes destroyed several homes, killing five civilians and injuring more than 30. According to the [Armed Conflict Location and Event Data Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_Conflict_Location_and_Event_Data_Project), while 4,800 of about 7,000 civilian fatalities have been caused by the Saudi-led coalition since 2016, the Houthis are accountable for 1,300 civilian deaths.[^cite_note-415]

According to a 2019 report by UNICEF, two million children had dropped out of school in Yemen since the conflict began in March 2015. The education of the other 3.7 million children was endangered because teachers had not been paid since 2017.[^cite_note-416]

On 9 October 2019, children's advocacy group, Save the Children warned of a significant rise in cholera cases in northern Yemen. The crisis caused by an increase in fuel shortages has affected several thousand children and their families.[^cite_note-417]

### Health

Main articles: [Health in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Yemen), [2016–2022 Yemen cholera outbreak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%932022_Yemen_cholera_outbreak), [COVID-19 pandemic in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Yemen), and [Famine in Yemen (2016–present)](51686838.md)

This section is an excerpt from [Health in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Yemen).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Health_in_Yemen\&action=edit)]
