A [hospital lab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_laboratory) in Aden



2015\. Water crisis in Sana'a

Much of Yemen's health care developments were undone after the escalation of conflict in 2015, which severely disrupted the delivery of health care.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:74-418][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:61-419][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:40-420][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:66-421] Damage to infrastructure and shortages of personnel and supplies caused a quick deterioration in [health indicators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_indicator) as documented by the [WBG](36752.md).[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:45-422][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:11-423][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_WHO-QualityStrategy2025-30-424][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_WB-HumanCapitalYemen-425] [United Nations](31769.md) agencies identify the ongoing food crisis as a major cause of poor health outcomes.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:1-426][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:100-427] In 2020, WHO and UN estimated that 45% or about 13.5 million people in Yemen required urgent nutritional assistance, including between 540.00 to 98.000 children under five with severe acute [malnutrition](258979.md).[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:100-427][^cite_note-428][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:43-429]



Working with children. Al Sabeen Hospital, [Sanaa](18691665.md)

Assessments indicate that the health system faces severe capacity limitations.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:11-423][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:43-429] According to WHO's 2024 [HeRAMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HeRAMS) survey, which assessed health facilities across 16 [governorates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governorate), 45% were fully functional, 38% partially functional, and 17% non functional.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:11-423][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:43-429][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_WHOEMRO2016-430][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_WHOHeRAMS2024PDF-431] The same survey calculated [inpatient care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inpatient_care) capacity to be approximately 6.2 hospital beds per 10,000 persons.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_WHOEMRO2016-430][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_WHOHeRAMS2024PDF-431] Health Cluster data from 2023 indicate that 21.9 million people required health assistance.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_IPCJanDec2023Snapshot-432] Workforce shortages are pronounced; 49 of 276 districts were reported to have no [physicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_doctor), while about 42% had two or fewer.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:101-433][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_HRH-Yemen-434][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_UniversalTruth-HRH-435] Estimates from the World Bank Group and WHO suggest a physician density of roughly 3 per 10,000 population.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:101-433] Maternal and child health services have been particularly affected, with international agencies reporting elevated maternal and [infant mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality) rates compared to global averages.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:102-436]

Yemen's health system remains constrained by [economic collapse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_collapse) and weak financial governance.[^cite_note-437][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_WHOYemen2026-438] Governance is fragmented, as rival authorities control different parts of the country.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:40-420][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:44-439][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:103-440][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:42-441][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:41-442] [Humanitarian organizations](1064581.md), including United Nations agencies, now deliver a large share of health services, along with essential support such as food and water.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_UNICEF-CHS-Yemen-443][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:71-444][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:72-445] [Noncommunicable diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-communicable_disease) account for more than half of all deaths.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:76-446][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:77-447][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:104-448] At the same time, outbreaks of [communicable diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infection) continue to occur, associated with displacement, reduced [vaccination](32473.md) coverage, and inadequate water and [sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanitation).[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:10-449][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:9-450][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:31-451] [Mental health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mental_health) needs are also high.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:46-452][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:47-453] In 2025 WBG counted close to 42 million people living in Yemen.[^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:105-454][^cite_note-Health_in_Yemen_\:106-455]

### Religious minorities

The US government estimates that religious minorities in Yemen constitute less than 1% of the population. They are categorized into four groups including Baha'is, Jews, Christians and Hindus. The Houthi movement has been said to be intolerant towards religious minorities. It was claimed that Houthis attachment to their own practices has led to a discriminatory stance against other types of faiths present in territories that are under their control. The Baha'i International Community stated that a number of Baha'is were imprisoned by the Houthi for religious reasons.[^cite_note-\:1-456]

In the province of Al-Bayda, AQAP was responsible for the execution of four people on 10 January 2019. The justification given related to their atheist beliefs.[^cite_note-\:1-456]

As of March 2020, the Jewish Cemetery in Aden was destroyed; as of April 2020, the fate of the last 50 Jews in Yemen was reported to be unknown.[^cite_note-457] On 13 July 2020 it was reported that the Houthi Militia is capturing the last Jews of Yemen of the [Kharif District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kharif_District).[^cite_note-458] On 16 July 2020, five Jews were allowed to leave Yemen by the Houthi, leaving 33 Jews in the country.[^cite_note-459] In July 2020, the Mona Relief reported on their website that as of 19 July 2020, of the Jewish population in Yemen there were only a "handful" of Jews in Sanaa.[^cite_note-460] On 29 March 2021, the [Iranian](14653.md)-backed [Houthi government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_movement) deported the last remaining [Yemenite Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemenite_Jews) to [Egypt](8087628.md), ending the continuous presence of a community that dated back to antiquity.[^cite_note-461]

### Education

The civil war in Yemen severely impacted and degraded the country's education system. The number of children who are out of school increased to 1.8 million in 2015–2016 out of more than 5 million registered students, according to 2013 statistics released by the Ministry of Education.[^cite_note-Moheyddeen-462] Moreover, 3600 schools are directly affected; 68 schools are occupied by armed groups, 248 schools have severe structural damage, and 270 are used to house refugees. The Yemen government has not been able to improve this situation due to limited authority and manpower.

Some of the education system's problems include: not enough financial resources to operate schools and salaries of the teachers, not enough materials to reconstruct damaged schools, and a lack of machinery to print textbooks and provide school supplies. These are caused by an unstable government that cannot offer enough financial support since many schools are either damaged or used for other purposes.[^cite_note-463]

Despite warfare and destruction of schools, the education ministry was able to send teams to oversee primary and secondary schools' final exams in order to give students 15–16 school year certificates.[^cite_note-Moheyddeen-462] Currently, [UNICEF](19698110.md) is raising money to support students and fix schools damaged by armed conflicts.

### Residential condition

The Yemeni quality of life is affected by the civil war and people have suffered enormous hardships. Although mines are banned by the government, Houthi forces placed anti-personnel mines in many parts of Yemen including Aden.[^cite_note-464] Thousands of civilians are injured when they accidentally step on mines; many lose their legs and injure their eyes. It is estimated that more than 500,000 mines have been laid by Houthi forces during the conflict. The pro-Hadi Yemen Army was able to remove 300,000 Houthi mines in recently captured areas, including 40,000 mines on the outskirts of [Marib](3604484.md) province, according to official sources.[^cite_note-465]

In addition, the nine-country coalition led by Saudi Arabia launched many airstrikes against Houthi forces; between March 2015 and December 2018 more than 4,600 civilians have been killed and much of the civilian infrastructure for goods and food production, storage, and distribution has been destroyed.[^cite_note-466][^cite_note-467] Factories have ceased production and thousands of people have lost their jobs. Due to decreased production, food, medicines, and other consumer staples have become scarce. The prices of these goods have gone up and civilians can no longer afford them for sustenance.[^cite_note-468]

### Refugees

[Djibouti](17207794.md), a small country in the [Horn of Africa](62142.md) across the [Bab-el-Mandeb](62138.md) strait from [Yemen](350939.md), has received an influx of refugees since the start of the campaign.[^cite_note-youssouf-469][^cite_note-470][^cite_note-471] Refugees also fled from Yemen to [Somalia](27358.md), arriving by sea in [Somaliland](51425.md) and [Puntland](233569.md) starting 28 March.[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-473] On 16 April 2015, 2,695 refugees of 48 nationalities were reported to have fled to [Oman](22316.md) in the past two weeks.[^cite_note-474]

According to Asyam Hafizh, an [Indonesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_people) student who was studying in Yemen, Al-Qaeda of Yemen has rescued at least 89 Indonesian civilians who were trapped in the conflict. Later on he arrived in Indonesia and he told his story to local media.[^cite_note-475] [United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees](31964.md) (UNHCR) reported in August 2015 that a total of almost 100,000 people had fled Yemen, especially to regional countries, like Saudi Arabia and [Djibouti](17207794.md).[^cite_note-476] In September 2016, the UNHCR estimated displacement of 2.4 million Yemenis within the country and 120,000 seeking asylum.[^cite_note-477]

In 2018, [500 refugees fleeing the civil war in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_on_Jeju_Island) came to [Jeju Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeju_Island), causing unease among the residents of Jeju Island.[^cite_note-478][^cite_note-479]

According to the International Organisation for Migration, despite the dangerous situation, nearly 150,000 migrants from Ethiopia arrived in Yemen in 2018, most of whom were on their way to Saudi Arabia in search of employment.[^cite_note-480]

In October 2019, [Kuwait](7515890.md) donated \$12 million to the UNHCR to support its humanitarian programs in Yemen. Salvatore Lombardo, Chief of Staff at office of the UNHCR, said that the donation will be allocated to address the issues of Yemen's [internally displaced persons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internally_displaced_persons) (IDPs).[^cite_note-481]

### Evacuation of foreign nationals from Yemen

See also: [Evacuation of Pakistani citizens during the Yemeni civil war (2015)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evacuation_of_Pakistani_citizens_during_the_Yemeni_civil_war_\(2015\)) and [Operation Raahat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Raahat)



Registration of [Indian](14533.md) citizens evacuating from Yemen in March 2015

The [Royal Saudi Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Saudi_Navy) evacuated diplomats and [United Nations](31769.md) staff from Aden to [Jeddah](277718.md) on 28 March 2015 as the Houthis targeted the city.[^cite_note-flee-482]

[Pakistan](23235.md) dispatched two special [PIA flights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_International_Airlines) to [evacuate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evacuation_of_Pakistani_citizens_from_Yemen_during_the_Yemeni_crises) from Aden some 500 [stranded Pakistanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistanis_in_Yemen) on 29 March 2015.[^cite_note-483] Several UN staff members and Arab diplomats were also evacuated following the airstrikes.[^cite_note-484]

The Indian government responded by deploying ships and planes to Yemen to evacuate stranded Indians. India began evacuating its citizens from Aden on 2 April by sea.[^cite_note-485] An air evacuation of Indian nationals from [Sanaa](18691665.md) to [Djibouti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djibouti_\(city\)) started on 3 April, after the Indian government obtained permission to land two [Airbus A320s](164933.md) at the Houthi-controlled [airport](4202141.md).[^cite_note-486] The [Indian Armed Forces](7171338.md) carried out a rescue operation codenamed *[Operation Raahat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Raahat)* and evacuated more than 4640 overseas Indians in Yemen along with 960 foreign nationals from 41 countries.[^cite_note-Kumar_2015-487] The Sanaa air evacuation ended on 9 April 2015 while the Aden evacuation by sea ended on 11 April 2015.[^cite_note-onein2015-488] The United States did not undertake an evacuation of private U.S. citizens from the country,[^cite_note-489] but some Americans (as well as Europeans) took part in an evacuation organized by the Indian government.[^cite_note-490]

A Chinese [missile frigate](11464.md) docked in Aden on 29 March to evacuate Chinese nationals.[^cite_note-491] The ship reportedly deployed soldiers ashore on 2 April to guard the evacuation of civilians from the city.[^cite_note-492] Hundreds of Chinese and other foreign nationals were safely evacuated aboard the frigate in the first operation of its kind carried out by the Chinese military.[^cite_note-493] The Philippines announced that 240 Filipinos that had grouped in Sanaa were evacuated across the Saudi border to [Jizan](1907222.md), before boarding flights to [Riyadh](51554.md) and then to [Manila](184334.md).[^cite_note-494]

The [Malaysian](3607937.md) government deployed two [Royal Malaysian Air Force](1137609.md) [C-130](7697.md) aircraft to evacuate their citizens from the safe airports in [Djibouti](17207794.md) and [Dubai](211583.md).[^cite_note-star-495] From Aden they were recuperated first by sea,[^cite_note-cna-496] and there were many in [Hadhramaut Governorate](3604476.md).[^cite_note-star-495] On 15 April, around 600 people were evacuated to Malaysia, also comprising citizens of other Southeast Asian countries such as 85 Indonesians, 9 Cambodians, 3 Thais and 2 Vietnamese.[^cite_note-cna-496]

The [Indonesian Air Force](8842734.md) also sent a [Boeing 737-400](15103676.md) and a chartered aircraft to evacuate Indonesian citizens. Indonesians remained in [Tarim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarim,_Yemen), [Mukallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mukallah), [Aden](38050.md), [Sanaa](18691665.md) and [Hudaydah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hudaydah).[^cite_note-497]

The [Ethiopian](187749.md) Foreign Ministry said it would airlift its citizens out of Yemen if they requested to be evacuated.[^cite_note-498] There were reportedly more than 50,000 Ethiopian nationals living and working in Yemen at the outbreak of hostilities.[^cite_note-ethiopia-499] More than 3,000 Ethiopians registered to evacuate from Yemen, and as of 17 April, the Ethiopian Foreign Ministry had confirmed 200 evacuees to date.[^cite_note-500]

Throughout April [Russian](25391.md) military forces evacuated more than 1,000 people of various nationalities, including primarily Russian citizens, on at least nine flights to [Chkalovsky Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chkalovsky_Airport), a military air base near [Moscow](19004.md). In early April around 900 had come from Houthi-controlled Sanaa, and the balance from Aden.[^cite_note-501]

The UNSC Resolution 2216 was then agreed on 14 April 2015.[^cite_note-unsc2216-502]

## United Nations response

The [United Nations](31769.md) has been closely following the Yemeni war since its beginning. It has looked for solutions to alleviate security concerns in Yemen and stop the Hadi-Houthi conflict from escalating. The Peace and National Partnership Agreement in 2014 was its final attempt at quelling the dispute before it transformed into a war.[^cite_note-\:0-130]

The United Nations representative [Baroness Amos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valerie_Amos,_Baroness_Amos), [Under-Secretary-General for Humanitarian Affairs and Emergency Relief Coordinator](1607007.md), said on 2 April 2015 that she was "extremely concerned" about the fate of civilians trapped in fierce fighting, after aid agencies reported 519 people killed and 1,700 injured in two weeks. The UN children's agency reported 62 children killed and 30 injured and also children being recruited as soldiers.[^cite_note-503]

[Russia](25391.md) called for "humanitarian pauses" in the coalition bombing campaign, bringing the idea before the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) in a 4 April emergency meeting.[^cite_note-504] However, Saudi Arabia's ambassador to the United Nations questioned whether humanitarian pauses would be the best way of delivering humanitarian assistance.[^cite_note-505]

On 22 March 2015, the UN declared its recognition of Hadi as the sole legitimate president and encouraged the peaceful resolution of the dispute. A few days later, Saudi Arabia intervened military with an air strike.[^cite_note-\:0-130]

On 14 April 2015, the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) adopted a resolution placing sanctions on [Abdul-Malik al-Houthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul-Malik_al-Houthi) and [Ahmed Ali Saleh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Ali_Saleh), establishing an [arms embargo](5974443.md) on the [Houthis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_movement), and calling on the Houthis to withdraw from Sanaa and other areas they seized.[^cite_note-506] The Houthis condemned the UN resolution and called for mass protests.[^cite_note-507]

[Jamal Benomar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamal_Benomar), the UN envoy to Yemen who brokered the deal that ended [Ali Abdullah Saleh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Abdullah_Saleh)'s presidency during the [2011–12 revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemeni_Revolution), resigned on 15 April.[^cite_note-508] Mauritanian diplomat [Ismail Ould Cheikh Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismail_Ould_Cheikh_Ahmed), formerly the head of the UN's Ebola response mission, was confirmed as the new UN Envoy to Yemen on 25 April.[^cite_note-509] The Panel of Experts on Yemen mandated by the [Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_Council), UN submitted a 329-page report to the latter's president on 26 January 2018 denouncing the UAE, the Yemeni government and the Houthis for torturing civilians in the Yemeni conflict.[^cite_note-510]

In December 2018, UN-sponsored talks between the Houthis and the Saudi-backed government were expected to start. The UN also started using its jets to carry wounded Houthi fighters out of the Yemeni capital, [Sanaa](18691665.md), to Oman, paving the way for planned peace talks after nearly four years of civil war.[^cite_note-511]

According to [United Nations](31769.md), more than 3.6 million Yemenis have been displaced in the 5-year-old conflict. The [World Food Programme](33582.md), which feeds more than 12 million Yemenis needs more funding to continue the ongoing operations and ramp back up operations in the north. Without funding, 30 of 41 major aid programmes in [Yemen](350939.md) would close in the next few weeks.[^cite_note-512]

On 27 September 2020, the United Nations announced that the Iran-backed Houthi rebels and the Hadi government supported by the Saudi-led military coalition, agreed to exchange about 1,081 detainees and prisoners related to the conflict as part of a release plan reached in early 2020. The deal stated the release of 681 rebels along with 400 Hadi government forces, which included fifteen Saudis and four Sudanese. The deal was finalized after a week-long meeting held in Glion, Switzerland, co-chaired by UN Special Envoy for Yemen, [Martin Griffiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Griffiths).[^cite_note-\:32-513] The prisoner-swap deal was done by the UN during the 2018 peace talks in Sweden which produced the Stockholm Agreement. The latter consisted of three major documents. The first one suggested the formation of a committee; the second called for the exchange of prisoners; the third aimed at creating the United Nations Mission to support the al-Hodeida Agreement (UNMHA).[^cite_note-\:0-130] Both parties agreed on several measures including a ceasefire in the strategic port city of Hodeida. A prisoner swap deal was made as part of the 2018 peace talks held in Sweden. However, the implementation of the plan clashed with military offensives from Houthis and the Saudi-led coalition, which aggravated the humanitarian crisis in Yemen, leaving millions suffering medical and food supply shortages.[^cite_note-\:32-513]

On 7 October 2022, the UN Human Rights Council adopted, without a vote, a resolution on Yemen which Human Rights Watch described as futile and failing to establish a credible independent and impartial monitoring and accountability mechanism. The adoption came a year after the same body rejected the renewal of the mandate for the Group of Eminent Experts (GEE), which the organization describes as an international, impartial, and independent body established by the Human Rights Council in 2017 that reported on rights violations and abuses in Yemen.[^cite_note-514]

## Peace process

Main article: [Yemeni peace process](34801006.md)

The Yemeni Civil War, which began in 2014, has been one of the most heavy conflicts in history, and caused a severe humanitarian crisis. In recent years, however, there have been several efforts to achieve a peaceful resolution and bringing stability to the country.[^cite_note-515]

One of the most important developments in the peace process was the Stockholm Agreement, which was reached in December 2018 between the conflicting parties, the government of Yemen and the Houthi rebels. This agreement focused on implementing a ceasefire in the city of [Hodeida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Hudaydah) and the areas surrounding the city. The agreement also focused on the redeployment of forces, and addressing humanitarian concerns. The agreement was seen as a significant step to alleviate the suffering of the Yemeni people and for further negotiations.[^cite_note-516]

Subsequently, there have been multiple rounds of talks and negotiations facilitated by the United Nations. These discussions have covered a comprehensive political solution, prisoner exchanges and the establishment of a transitional government. The main goal has been to address the causes of the conflict, foster national reconciliation and restore security and stability in Yemen.[^cite_note-517]

Another important development in the peace process was the [Riyadh Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riyadh_Agreement), between the Yemeni government and the [Southern Transitional Council](54251566.md) (STC) in November 2019. This agreement aimed to resolve power struggles in southern Yemen by establishing a power-sharing arrangement between the two parties. It was seen as a significant step towards achieving a unified Yemen and an important component of the broader peace process.[^cite_note-518] While progress has been made, the road to peace in Yemen remains challenging due to ongoing clashes, political divisions and regional rivalries. Additionally, the humanitarian situation in Yemen remains dire, with millions of people in urgent need of assistance.[^cite_note-519]

*[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* reported in March 2023 that Iran agreed to halt all military support to Houthis and abide by the [UN arms embargo](5974443.md), as part of a Chinese-brokered [Iran-Saudi rapprochement deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_proxy_conflict#Iran-Saudi_Arabia_Rapprochement). The agreement was concluded with the objective of putting pressure on Houthi militants to end the conflict through negotiated settlement; with Saudi and US officials describing the concomitant Iranian behaviour as a "litmus test" for the endurance of the Chinese-brokered [détente](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C3%A9tente). The agreement was concluded in the background of Iran's international isolation following the escalation of its [nuclear program](721807.md), an ongoing crackdown on [Mahsa Amini protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahsa_Amini_protests) and its [support to Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_and_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-520][^cite_note-521][^cite_note-522][^cite_note-523]

On May 14, 2026, following 14 weeks of negotiations in [Amman](45007.md), [Jordan](7515964.md), with various UN officials and [International Committee of the Red Cross](5023832.md) (ICRC) observers, both governments sign a deal to release nearly 1,700 detainees in the largest [prisoner swap](17209653.md) in the war's history. The Houthi head of the Prisoner's Affairs committee said that 1,100 of the detainees are Houthi-affiliated, while the remaining 580 from the other side include seven Saudis and 20 Sudanese nationals. The ICRC was chosen to facilitate the [repatriation](1481067.md) process for both sides.[^cite_note-524]

## Other developments

Armed Houthis ransacked [Al Jazeera](30204052.md)'s news bureau in Sanaa on 27 March 2015, amid [Qatar](8238258.md)'s participation in [military intervention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabian-led_intervention_in_Yemen) against the group. The Qatar-based news channel condemned the attack on its bureau.[^cite_note-voa-525] On 28 March, [Ali Abdullah Saleh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Abdullah_Saleh) stated neither he nor anyone in his family would run for president, despite recent campaigning by his supporters for his son [Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Saleh) to seek the presidency. He also called on [Abdrabbuh Mansur Hadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdrabbuh_Mansur_Hadi) to step down as president and said new elections should be held.[^cite_note-526]

Rumors about Saleh's whereabouts swirled during the conflict. Foreign Minister [Riad Yassin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riad_Yassin), a Hadi loyalist, claimed on 4 April that Saleh left Yemen aboard a Russian aircraft [evacuating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_reactions_to_the_Saudi-led_intervention_in_Yemen_\(2015%E2%80%93present\)) foreign nationals from [Sanaa International Airport](4202141.md).[^cite_note-provinces-527] Later in the month, Saleh reportedly asked the Saudi-led coalition for a "safe exit" for himself and his family, but the request was turned down.[^cite_note-528]

[King Salman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salman_of_Saudi_Arabia) reshuffled the Saudi cabinet on 28 April, removing [Prince Muqrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muqrin_of_Saudi_Arabia) as his designated successor. The Saudi royal palace said Muqrin had asked to step down, without giving a reason, but media speculation was that Muqrin did not demonstrate sufficient support for the Saudi-led military campaign in Yemen.[^cite_note-529] A spokesman for Yemen's exiled government told [Reuters](18998750.md) on 29 April that the country would officially seek membership in the [Gulf Cooperation Council](220950.md).[^cite_note-530] Media reports have noted that the civil war has reached nearly all of Yemen, with one notable exception being the remote [Indian Ocean](14580.md) archipelago of [Socotra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socotra),[^cite_note-531] where the war spread due to the [South Yemen insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Yemen_insurgency) in 2017.[^cite_note-UAEBacked-532]

On 30 September 2019, Houthi rebels released 290 Yemeni prisoners in a move, stated by the United Nations as a revival of peace process, the International Committee of the Red Cross (ICRC) said.[^cite_note-533]

In September 2020, the United Nations announced a swap of "1081 conflict-related prisoners" between the two opponents, including Saudi and Sudanese troops fighting for the Saudi-led coalition.[^cite_note-534] In October 2020, two American hostages Sandra Loli and Mikael Gidada who had been captives for 16 months, were freed by the Houthi rebels in exchange for releasing 240 former Houthi combatants in Oman.[^cite_note-535]

In January 2021, the [US Secretary of State](32293.md) [Mike Pompeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Pompeo) announced his intentions of declaring the [Houthi movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_movement) in Yemen a "foreign terrorist organization". Under the plan, the three leaders of the Houthis (known as Ansar Allah) were to be listed as Specially Designated Global Terrorists. However, it induced a fear among diplomats and aid groups that the move would cause issues in the peace talks and in delivering aid to the Yemen crisis.[^cite_note-536]

Although the United Arab Emirates had announced plans in 2019 to vacate Yemen and withdraw all its troops, the Arab nation continued to maintain its presence for strategic objectives. Reports in May 2021 revealed that the Emirates was building a mysterious airbase on Yemen's [Mayun Island](1907645.md) in the [Bab el-Mandeb Strait](62138.md). Satellite images revealed that construction work to build a 1.85 km (6,070 ft) runway, along with three hangars, on the island was completed on 18 May 2021. Military officials of the Yemeni government said that the UAE was transporting military equipment, weapons and troops to Mayun Island through ships. The island was considered a strategic location for anyone who controls it, offering power for operations into the [Gulf of Aden](58716.md), the [Red Sea](26191.md) and [East Africa](260987.md), and for easily launching airstrikes into Yemen's mainland.[^cite_note-537]

On 7 October 2021, [Bahrain](18933277.md), along with [Russia](25391.md) and other members of the [UN human rights council](635790.md), opposed the council's investigations into the war crimes in Yemen. The members narrowly voted to reject the [Netherlands'](21148.md) resolution to give another two years to independent investigators to continue the probe to monitor atrocities in the Yemen war. In the past, independent investigators concluded that potential war crimes have been committed by all sides. An ally of Bahrain, [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) is not a voting member of the council, but rights activists say the Kingdom heavily lobbied against the resolution.[^cite_note-538]

On 20 March 2022, the [United Nations](31769.md) and the [International Committee of the Red Cross](5023832.md) reported that the [Yemeni government](78626.md) and the [Houthis](17240332.md) agreed to release 887 detainees, following 10 days of negotiations in [Switzerland](26748.md). Both parties also agreed to visitation rights in detention facilities and likely more prisoner swaps in the near future. [Hans Grundberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Grundberg), the UN's special envoy for Yemen said that things are finally moving "in the right direction" toward a resolution of the conflict. This came after the recent [Saudi-Iranian rapprochement](19661342.md) mediated by China a week earlier.[^cite_note-539]

In December 2025 following talks in Muscat, Oman, Yemen's internationally recognized government and the Houthi movement, agreed on the exchange of 2900–3000 detainees held by both sides, including some Saudi and Sudanese nationals.[^cite_note-540]

## Environmental impact

### Water

Yemen is facing one of the world's worst Water, Sanitation, and Hygiene (WASH) crises. The lack of governance has left Yemen without a viable water supply. Poor sanitation and the lack of clean water has had a deteriorating effect on the health of Yemenis, which is apparent through the increasing cases of cholera in Yemen since 2015. The entire country has been affected by a water shortage and the price of drinking water has more than doubled. Drinking water has become unaffordable for most Yemenis. The problem in Yemen is widespread, thus making it difficult to prevent the problem from escalating because it is hard to supply everyone on a regular basis.

The Regional Director for the Middle East and North Africa at UNICEF, Geert Cappelaere, has explained that fuel shortages in Yemen have deepened the water and health crisis. The water pumping stations in Yemen have been jeopardized as they are quickly running out of fuel and over 3 million people are dependent on these water pumps which have been established through public networks. The ICRC has been working closely with vulnerable people where resources are limited but aims to prevent the water crisis from worsening by buying 750,000 liters of diesel to provide clean water for people living in Yemen. Already a scarce commodity, the amount of water withdrawn from wells in 2016 reached unsustainable levels. The ICRC has also been working carefully with local authorities to provide water to 330,000 people in Aden. They have also installed wells nearby Aden to provide water to the neighborhood when they are experiencing water shortages.

Water resources have been used by both sides during the war as a tactic during the conflict. Unlike other countries in the Middle East, Yemen has no rivers to depend on for water resources. In 2017, 250,000 people of Taiz's total population of 654,330 were served by public water supply networks. As a result of the ongoing conflict NGO's have struggled to reach the sanitation facilities due to security issues. Since the aerial bombardment, Taiz has been left in a critical situation as current water production is not sufficient for the population.

[Water availability in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Yemen) has decreased.[^cite_note-541] Water scarcity with an intrinsic geographical formation in the highlands and limited capital to build water infrastructure and provision services caused a catastrophic water shortage in Yemen. Aquifer recharge rates are decreasing while salt water intrusion is increasing.[^cite_note-542] After the civil war began in 2015, water buckets were destroyed significantly and the price of water highly increased. Storing water capacity has been demolished by war and supply chains have been occupied by military personnel, which makes the delivery of water far more difficult. In 2015, over 15 million people needed healthcare and over 20 million needed clean water and sanitation—an increase of 52 percent since the intervention, but the government agencies could not afford to deliver clean water to displaced Yemeni citizens.[^cite_note-543]

### Agriculture

The Yemen civil war resulted in a severe lack of food and vegetation. Agricultural production in the country has suffered substantially leaving Yemen to face the threat of famine. Yemen has been since [UNSC resolution 2216](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2216) was approved in April 2015,[^cite_note-unsc2216-502] under blockade by land, sea, and air which has disrupted the delivery of many foreign resources to Houthi-controlled territory. In a country where 90% of the food requirements are met through imports, this blockade has had serious consequences concerning the availability of food to its citizens.[^cite_note-Rageh-544] It is reported that out of the population of 24 million in Yemen, everyday 13 million are going hungry and 6 million are at risk of starvation.[^cite_note-Rageh-544] In October 2016, [Robert Fisk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Fisk) reported that there is strong evidence suggesting that the agricultural sector in Houthi-controlled territory was being deliberately destroyed by the Saudi-led coalition, thus exacerbating the food shortage and leaving the Houthis dependent solely on imports, which are difficult to obtain in view of the blockade.[^cite_note-fisk16-545]
